  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="iii-crystalizing-plans-for-diplomacy" class="entity-list-title">III. Crystalizing Plans for Diplomacy</h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rq33sp-s6br8/Doc-10-1963-01-03.pdf.jpeg?itok=d4SE7FfA" alt="Doc-10-1963-01-03" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 10</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29782-document-10-steering-group-implementing-nassau-decisions-minutes-2nd-meeting-held" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Steering Group on Implementing the Nassau Decisions, “Minutes of 2nd Meeting Held January 3, 1963, at 5:00 P.M.,” 3 January 1963, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-03T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 3, 1963</time>
</div>cret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-03T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 3, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Conference Files, box 307, CF 2217 Steering Group on Implementing the Nassau Decisions 12/1962-2/1963, Sub Misc (folder 1 of 2)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The Nassau Steering Group devoted its January 3, 1963, session to Jupiter removal diplomacy. Ambassadors Finletter, Hare and Reinhardt were present as well as McGeorge Bundy and Defense Department General Counsel John McNaughton. While the papers on the Jupiter that the committee prepared remain classified, the discussion summarized here covered some of the key issues. One was to avoid the word “withdrawal” when discussing the Jupiters and to use the word “replace” instead (as in “replace” Jupiters with Polaris SLBMs). Moreover, because of concern about leaks, there would be no reference to an April 1, 1963, deadline in communications with the Italians and Turks. As April 1 would be six months after the Cuban crisis, State Department official Seymour Weiss wanted to “go to the mat” to keep any dates out of the official discussions because he worried that too much specificity would raise suspicions of a “deal” or would sound like an “ultimatum.”<a href="#_edn13" name="_ednref13">[13]</a> Nevertheless, an April 1 date would be used for the timing of the stationing of Polaris submarines in the Mediterranean, and some U.S. interlocutors would see it as a deadline.</p>cret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-03T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 3, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Conference Files, box 307, CF 2217 Steering Group on Implementing the Nassau Decisions 12/1962-2/1963, Sub Misc (folder 1 of 2)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The Nassau Steering Group devoted its January 3, 1963, session to Jupiter removal diplomacy. Ambassadors Finletter, Hare and Reinhardt were present as well as McGeorge Bundy and Defense Department General Counsel John McNaughton. While the papers on the Jupiter that the committee prepared remain classified, the discussion summarized here covered some of the key issues. One was to avoid the word “withdrawal” when discussing the Jupiters and to use the word “replace” instead (as in “replace” Jupiters with Polaris SLBMs). Moreover, because of concern about leaks, there would be no reference to an April 1, 1963, deadline in communications with the Italians and Turks. As April 1 would be six months after the Cuban crisis, State Department official Seymour Weiss wanted to “go to the mat” to keep any dates out of the official discussions because he worried that too much specificity would raise suspicions of a “deal” or would sound like an “ultimatum.”<a href="#_edn13" name="_ednref13">[13]</a> Nevertheless, an April 1 date would be used for the timing of the stationing of Polaris submarines in the Mediterranean, and some U.S. interlocutors would see it as a deadline.</p>_ednref13">[13]</a> Nevertheless, an April 1 date would be used for the timing of the stationing of Polaris submarines in the Mediterranean, and some U.S. interlocutors would see it as a deadline.</p>

<p>The Steering Group also addressed the problems raised by the early deployment to Turkey of F-104Gs; making the fighter-bombers available by May 1963 would require the rerouting of planes that had already been assigned to the Republic of China (Taiwan), Denmark, Norway, and Greece. There would be a delay in deploying nuclear bombs for the F-104s until they were outfitted with Permissive Action Links (PALs), as required by President Kennedy, which was not likely to occur until later in the year.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 11</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29783-document-11-letter-secretary-defense-mcnamara-defense-minister-giulio-andreotti-5" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Letter, Secretary of Defense McNamara to Defense Minister Giulio Andreotti, 5 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-05T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 5, 1963</time>
</div>_ednref13">[13]</a> Nevertheless, an April 1 date would be used for the timing of the stationing of Polaris submarines in the Mediterranean, and some U.S. interlocutors would see it as a deadline.</p>

<p>The Steering Group also addressed the problems raised by the early deployment to Turkey of F-104Gs; making the fighter-bombers available by May 1963 would require the rerouting of planes that had already been assigned to the Republic of China (Taiwan), Denmark, Norway, and Greece. There would be a delay in deploying nuclear bombs for the F-104s until they were outfitted with Permissive Action Links (PALs), as required by President Kennedy, which was not likely to occur until later in the year.</p>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 11</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29783-document-11-letter-secretary-defense-mcnamara-defense-minister-giulio-andreotti-5" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Letter, Secretary of Defense McNamara to Defense Minister Giulio Andreotti, 5 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-05T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 5, 1963</time>
</div>cret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-05T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 5, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Foreign Service Posts, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Members of the Nassau Decisions Steering Group worked up the texts of letters from Secretary of Defense Robert McNamara to the Italian and Turkish Defense Ministers, which President Kennedy approved when he <a href="https://jfklibrary.libguides.com/ld.php?content_id=26059410">met</a> with Dean Rusk and Thomas Finletter in Palm Beach on January 5, 1963 (see document 10). The State Department sent the letters to Ankara and Rome later that day.</p>cret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-05T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 5, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Foreign Service Posts, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Members of the Nassau Decisions Steering Group worked up the texts of letters from Secretary of Defense Robert McNamara to the Italian and Turkish Defense Ministers, which President Kennedy approved when he <a href="https://jfklibrary.libguides.com/ld.php?content_id=26059410">met</a> with Dean Rusk and Thomas Finletter in Palm Beach on January 5, 1963 (see document 10). The State Department sent the letters to Ankara and Rome later that day.</p>ld.php?content_id=26059410">met</a> with Dean Rusk and Thomas Finletter in Palm Beach on January 5, 1963 (see document 10). The State Department sent the letters to Ankara and Rome later that day.</p>

<p>McNamara’s letters expand on the points about the need to replace Jupiters with Polaris missiles that he made to Andreotti and Sancar when he met them in Paris. To both, McNamara wrote that the Polaris force would be “on station” by April 1 as the replacement for the Jupiters. Writing to Andreotti, he also mentioned substituting “obsolete” <a href="https://history.redstone.army.mil/miss-corporal.html#:~:text=Known%20as%20the%20embryo%20of,deployed%20in%20Europe%20in%201955.">Corporal</a> with <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MGM-29_Sergeant">Sergeant</a> missiles. In his <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d386">message</a> to Sancar, McNamara informed him that he was exploring the possibility of accelerated delivery of the F-104s and that “emergency actions” could make it possible to deliver the first squadron during April 1963.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 12</p>
</div>ld.php?content_id=26059410">met</a> with Dean Rusk and Thomas Finletter in Palm Beach on January 5, 1963 (see document 10). The State Department sent the letters to Ankara and Rome later that day.</p>

<p>McNamara’s letters expand on the points about the need to replace Jupiters with Polaris missiles that he made to Andreotti and Sancar when he met them in Paris. To both, McNamara wrote that the Polaris force would be “on station” by April 1 as the replacement for the Jupiters. Writing to Andreotti, he also mentioned substituting “obsolete” <a href="https://history.redstone.army.mil/miss-corporal.html#:~:text=Known%20as%20the%20embryo%20of,deployed%20in%20Europe%20in%201955.">Corporal</a> with <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MGM-29_Sergeant">Sergeant</a> missiles. In his <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d386">message</a> to Sancar, McNamara informed him that he was exploring the possibility of accelerated delivery of the F-104s and that “emergency actions” could make it possible to deliver the first squadron during April 1963.</p>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 12</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29784-document-12-jan-8-1963" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">State Department telegram 1241 to U.S. Embassy Italy, 8 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 8, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Foreign Service Posts, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 120, 320.1 NATO</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In this “limited distribution” message, Under Secretary of State George Ball informed Ambassador Reinhardt of the developing plans to deploy three Polaris boats in the Mediterranean with the missiles on station by April 1. Polaris would supersede the less effective Jupiters. The Ambassador should seek agreement with the Italian Government to take the “necessary steps” to dismantle the two Jupiter squadrons. The Italians “may be quite willing” to move in this direction, Rusk suggested, having already shown they recognized the value of replacing Jupiters with Polaris. Reinhardt should advise the Italians that the U.S. government was treating the matter with “great secrecy” and was making a parallel approach to Ankara.</p>>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 12</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29784-document-12-jan-8-1963" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">State Department telegram 1241 to U.S. Embassy Italy, 8 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-08T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 8, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Foreign Service Posts, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 120, 320.1 NATO</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In this “limited distribution” message, Under Secretary of State George Ball informed Ambassador Reinhardt of the developing plans to deploy three Polaris boats in the Mediterranean with the missiles on station by April 1. Polaris would supersede the less effective Jupiters. The Ambassador should seek agreement with the Italian Government to take the “necessary steps” to dismantle the two Jupiter squadrons. The Italians “may be quite willing” to move in this direction, Rusk suggested, having already shown they recognized the value of replacing Jupiters with Polaris. Reinhardt should advise the Italians that the U.S. government was treating the matter with “great secrecy” and was making a parallel approach to Ankara.</p>e value of replacing Jupiters with Polaris. Reinhardt should advise the Italians that the U.S. government was treating the matter with “great secrecy” and was making a parallel approach to Ankara.</p>

<p>The Italians would not be surprised by this initiative in light of Andreotti-McNamara discussions at the recent NATO meeting, which also covered U.S. interest in modernizing tactical nuclear delivery systems (Sergeant for Corporal missiles). U.S. views would also be repeated to Fanfani when he visited Washington, D.C.</p>

<p>The day before, and probably as a related move, President Kennedy invited Italian Prime Minister Amintore Fanfani for talks in Washington. During mid-December, Fanfani had expressed <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v13/d310">interest</a> in speaking with Kennedy about the world situation, and a meeting would provide an opportunity for the President to secure a commitment on the Jupiter issue.<a href="#_edn14" name="_ednref14">[14]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>At the conclusion of this memorandum, Kitchen noted that President Kennedy approved the “plan of action” on the Jupiters on January 5, 1963. This was a reference to Kennedy’s Palm Beach meeting with Rusk and Finletter. As Kitchen observed, the letters to Andreotti and Sancar went out on January 5, and messages to Ambassadors Hare and Reinhardt were sent a few days later. The <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d387">instructions</a> to Hare are in the FRUS, and the ones to Reinhardt precede this document<em>.</em></p>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 14</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29786-document-14-us-embassy-italy-telegram-1327-state-department-9-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy Italy Telegram 1327 to State Department, 9 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-09T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 9, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>abel">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Following his instructions to begin reaching a deal, Reinhardt met with Andreotti. The latter asked Reinhardt “whether the April 1 date was important,” noting that it would be better to remove the missiles after April 1 so it would not become an election issue, where the right could “condemn a great defeat,” while the left could claim a “great victory.” Reinhardt declared that Washington wanted the action on Jupiters taken “as soon as possible,” which Andreotti accepted, observing that an “optimum solution” would be a “two key” arrangement for Polaris, which he realized was not practical in the short term.</p>

<p>When Andreotti noted that the decommissioning of the Jupiter sites would be a “graphic step backward” for Italy in terms of its direct participation in nuclear defense, Reinhardt mentioned the replacement of Corporals with Sergeant missiles, which Andreotti acknowledged would be “helpful” for demonstrating a U.S. “presence” in Italy.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29787-document-15-us-embassy-turkey-telegram-765-state-department-12-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy Turkey telegram 765 to State Department, 12 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-12T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 12, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 2047, 782.56311/1-1263</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Ambassador Hare met with Turkish Foreign Minister Feridun Erkin about moving ahead on substituting Jupiter missiles with Polaris, emphasizing the “importance in Turkey’s getting in on ground floor of this significant move toward a stronger NATO.” Erkin implied that he was “impressed by the reasonableness of our proposals,” according to Hare, but believed that the public would have to be persuaded “that Polaris would be as effective as Jupiters in assuring security of Turkey.” Hare observed that visits to Turkish ports by Polaris submarines armed with ballistic missiles could help maintain Turkey’s “confidence” in the U.S. deterrent and in NATO.</p>
</div>

</div>il/public/documents/rq33sp-s6br8/Doc-15-1963-01-12.pdf.jpeg?itok=_AuDegNb" alt="Doc-15-1963-01-12" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29788-document-16-s-embassy-italy-telegram-1367-state-department-14-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy Italy telegram 1367 to State Department, 14 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-14T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 14, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 1924, 765.56311/1-1463</p>
</div>
          </div>eld--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 1924, 765.56311/1-1463</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>When Reinhardt met with President Antonio Segni, a Christian Democrat, the latter accepted the reasons for replacing Jupiters with Polaris, but said the Jupiter removal had “grave psychological and political implications” because the missiles had been a “symbol of Italian determination” to take part in the defense of the West. Worried that there was “too much neutralism” in Italy, Segni was concerned about the electoral impact of the Jupiter decision and believed that the “operation should be carried out in such a fashion as to leave no inference of a lessening of Italian participation in nuclear defense.”</p>
</div>

</div>

  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="iv-the-fanfani-visit-and-italian-suspicions-of-a-secret-deal" class="entity-list-title">IV. The Fanfani Visit and Italian Suspicions of a Secret Deal</h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rq33sp-s6br8/Doc-17-1963-01-16.pdf.jpeg?itok=v9ejDhOl" alt="Doc-17-1963-01-16" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">eld--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 1924, 765.56311/1-1463</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>When Reinhardt met with President Antonio Segni, a Christian Democrat, the latter accepted the reasons for replacing Jupiters with Polaris, but said the Jupiter removal had “grave psychological and political implications” because the missiles had been a “symbol of Italian determination” to take part in the defense of the West. Worried that there was “too much neutralism” in Italy, Segni was concerned about the electoral impact of the Jupiter decision and believed that the “operation should be carried out in such a fashion as to leave no inference of a lessening of Italian participation in nuclear defense.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
          </div>

</div>
  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="iv-the-fanfani-visit-and-italian-suspicions-of-a-secret-deal" class="entity-list-title">IV. The Fanfani Visit and Italian Suspicions of a Secret Deal</h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rq33sp-s6br8/Doc-17-1963-01-16.pdf.jpeg?itok=v9ejDhOl" alt="Doc-17-1963-01-16" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 17</p>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29789-document-17-memorandum-conversation-modernization-nuclear-missiles-italy-and" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conversation, “Modernization of Nuclear Missiles in Italy and the Mediterranean,” 16 January 1963, with State Department routing slip attached, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 17</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29789-document-17-memorandum-conversation-modernization-nuclear-missiles-italy-and" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Memorandum of Conversation, “Modernization of Nuclear Missiles in Italy and the Mediterranean,” 16 January 1963, with State Department routing slip attached, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-16T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 16, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964 (also published in <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v13/d312">FRUS</a>)</p>
</div>
          </div>ds 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964 (also published in <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v13/d312">FRUS</a>)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Prime Minister Fanfani’s visit to the U.S. was an opportunity to reach “a meeting of the minds” with President Kennedy on the Jupiter-Polaris problem. The two had several conversations during the next two days on East-West relations, NATO nuclear issues, and the developing world, among other topics.<a href="#_edn15" name="_ednref15">[15]</a> During this conversation, with only the U.S. translator present, Kennedy explained to Fanfani that Polaris/Sergeant missiles as a replacement for Jupiter/Corporals, along with Italian participation in an eventual MLF, should be announced “as a whole package” rather than “to have the different points of decision simply leak out, without coherence and possibly at the wrong moment.” He believed that the main elements of the agreement would find “general approval” among most political groupings in Italy. When Fanfani brought up the possibility of announcing the U.S. request on Jupiters and Polaris and then taking it to his government, Kennedy emphasized the need for quick action, adding that “it would not be desirable to allow for prolonged discussion” of the package.</p>ds 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964 (also published in <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v13/d312">FRUS</a>)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Prime Minister Fanfani’s visit to the U.S. was an opportunity to reach “a meeting of the minds” with President Kennedy on the Jupiter-Polaris problem. The two had several conversations during the next two days on East-West relations, NATO nuclear issues, and the developing world, among other topics.<a href="#_edn15" name="_ednref15">[15]</a> During this conversation, with only the U.S. translator present, Kennedy explained to Fanfani that Polaris/Sergeant missiles as a replacement for Jupiter/Corporals, along with Italian participation in an eventual MLF, should be announced “as a whole package” rather than “to have the different points of decision simply leak out, without coherence and possibly at the wrong moment.” He believed that the main elements of the agreement would find “general approval” among most political groupings in Italy. When Fanfani brought up the possibility of announcing the U.S. request on Jupiters and Polaris and then taking it to his government, Kennedy emphasized the need for quick action, adding that “it would not be desirable to allow for prolonged discussion” of the package.</p>n Jupiters and Polaris and then taking it to his government, Kennedy emphasized the need for quick action, adding that “it would not be desirable to allow for prolonged discussion” of the package.</p>

<p>At Fanfani’s request, Kennedy explained the arrangements for Polaris missions in the Mediterranean, which operated out of a base on the Iberian Peninsula (Rota, Spain), and the various options for an MLF, either surface or submarine ships. Such an approach, Kennedy believed, was a way to improve the “position of the West.” Accepting Kennedy’s assertions about the dangers of the Jupiter missiles, Fanfani nevertheless saw a “psychological” problem involving the “prestige and strength” of Italy’s armed forces. Kennedy “indicated lively interest” in Fanfani’s question as to whether the Jupiter bases could be used for “cooperative peaceful space efforts.”</p>

<p>At the meeting’s conclusion, Kennedy “stressed that by the following morning they should be able to combine four or five points into a proposal that would strengthen the Italian and American position within the framework of the Alliance, thus making this meeting a gain in its cohesiveness and hence political strength.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>

</div>n Jupiters and Polaris and then taking it to his government, Kennedy emphasized the need for quick action, adding that “it would not be desirable to allow for prolonged discussion” of the package.</p>

<p>At Fanfani’s request, Kennedy explained the arrangements for Polaris missions in the Mediterranean, which operated out of a base on the Iberian Peninsula (Rota, Spain), and the various options for an MLF, either surface or submarine ships. Such an approach, Kennedy believed, was a way to improve the “position of the West.” Accepting Kennedy’s assertions about the dangers of the Jupiter missiles, Fanfani nevertheless saw a “psychological” problem involving the “prestige and strength” of Italy’s armed forces. Kennedy “indicated lively interest” in Fanfani’s question as to whether the Jupiter bases could be used for “cooperative peaceful space efforts.”</p>

<p>At the meeting’s conclusion, Kennedy “stressed that by the following morning they should be able to combine four or five points into a proposal that would strengthen the Italian and American position within the framework of the Alliance, thus making this meeting a gain in its cohesiveness and hence political strength.”</p>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 18</p>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29790-document-18-fanfani-diary-entry-16-january-1963" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Fanfani Diary Entry, 16 January 1963</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-16T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 16, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Archivio Storico del Senato della repubblica Italiana, Amintore Fanfani, Diari, vol. IV, 1960-1963 (translation by Leopoldo Nuti)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Fanfani covered the events of the day in this journal entry, noting that he told Kennedy he would make no commitment on the Jupiter-Polaris replacement until he spoke with McNamara. With McNamara, Fanfani emphasized the point about using the Jupiter base for space launches. Both Kennedy and McNamara denied that there was a trade with the USSR, with the latter emphasizing the risks posed by the Jupiters during the Missile Crisis.</p>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 19</p>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29791-document-19-mcgeorge-bundy-secretary-state-rusk-16-january-1963-top-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">McGeorge Bundy to Secretary of State Rusk, 16 January 1963, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-16T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 16, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Executive Secretariat Conference Files, box 308, CF 2222</p>
</div>
          </div>g field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Executive Secretariat Conference Files, box 308, CF 2222</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>During the luncheon for Fanfani, Bundy sent Rusk this short memo about the Fanfani-Kennedy conversation, noting the former’s concern that removing the Jupiters could lead to attacks from the right about “softness toward left-wingers who want the missiles out.” When Kennedy spoke with McNamara after the meeting, he asked him to emphasize to Fanfani the military advantages of replacing the Jupiters with Polaris. McNamara wanted to emphasize that very point to offset any talk of a “nefarious Cuban bargain” with the Soviets. Bundy also highlighted the debate between George Ball and McNamara&nbsp;“as to whether Jupiters themselves should be mentioned in the communique” on the Fanfani-Kennedy discussions.</p>
</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 20</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29792-document-20-fanfani-diary-entry-17-january-1963-excerpt" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Fanfani Diary Entry, 17 January 1963 (excerpt)</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-17T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 17, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Archivio Storico del Senato della repubblica Italiana, Amintore Fanfani, Diari, vol. IV, 1960-1963 (translation by Leopoldo Nuti)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In the morning, Fanfani met with his advisers, and they agreed to accept the Jupiter-Polaris arrangement. He then met with Kennedy for a series of conversations on East-West issues and the developing countries. During their private meeting, Fanfani conveyed to Kennedy the conditions for the agreement—Polaris submarines would not be based in Italy, and Italy would be both a participant in the MLF and a member of the NATO committee establishing it. As he noted in his diary, Fanfani asked that language referring to “bases in the Mediterranean” (“which could lead one to suppose they are in Italy”) be removed from the “minute of understanding” of the meeting. Kennedy accepted the stipulations, and a memorandum of their understanding was prepared.</p>
</div>

</div>>

</div>

</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In the morning, Fanfani met with his advisers, and they agreed to accept the Jupiter-Polaris arrangement. <div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-17T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 17, 1963</time>

          </div>to suppose they are in Italy”) be removed from the “minute of understanding” of the meeting. Kennedy accepted the stipulations, and a memorandum of their understanding was prepared.</p>
</div>

</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 22</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29794-document-22-us-embassy-italy-telegram-1411-state-department-17-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy Italy telegram 1411 to State Department, 17 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-17T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 17, 1963</time>
</div>ret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-17T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 17, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>ret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-17T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 17, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>abel">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In mid-January 1963, Harvard University professor Henry Kissinger met in Rome with senior Italian political leaders<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—</span></span></span>all the way up to Fanfani and Segni<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—to&nbsp;</span></span></span>discuss U.S.-Italian relations, including the Jupiters. At that point, Kissinger had no official role in government, although during 1961-1962 he had been a White House consultant. According to his report to the Embassy, the Italian leadership understood “intellectually” why the U.S. wanted to remove the missiles but it was sorry that Italy was losing its “one-up” position among non-nuclear members of NATO. (No one mentioned that Italy retained special status as a country that the U.S. had to consult before it used nuclear weapons based there.) Segni felt some “pique” that the Jupiter decision had been made during the missile crisis and that three months had passed before his government learned of it. “Almost everyone,” according to Kissinger, “suspected that withdrawal might be [the] result of [a] US agreement with Russians,” with the April 1 deadline seen as an important clue.<a href="#_edn16" name="_ednref16">[16]</a></p>abel">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In mid-January 1963, Harvard University professor Henry Kissinger met in Rome with senior Italian political leaders<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—</span></span></span>all the way up to Fanfani and Segni<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—to&nbsp;</span></span></span>discuss U.S.-Italian relations, including the Jupiters. At that point, Kissinger had no official role in government, although during 1961-1962 he had been a White House consultant. According to his report to the Embassy, the Italian leadership understood “intellectually” why the U.S. wanted to remove the missiles but it was sorry that Italy was losing its “one-up” position among non-nuclear members of NATO. (No one mentioned that Italy retained special status as a country that the U.S. had to consult before it used nuclear weapons based there.) Segni felt some “pique” that the Jupiter decision had been made during the missile crisis and that three months had passed before his government learned of it. “Almost everyone,” according to Kissinger, “suspected that withdrawal might be [the] result of [a] US agreement with Russians,” with the April 1 deadline seen as an important clue.<a href="#_edn16" name="_ednref16">[16]</a></p>g to Kissinger, “suspected that withdrawal might be [the] result of [a] US agreement with Russians,” with the April 1 deadline seen as an important clue.<a href="#_edn16" name="_ednref16">[16]</a></p>

<p>The U.S. Embassy report on Kissinger’s findings arrived at the State Department the morning of January 17, 1963, with instructions for the Executive Secretariat to limit its distribution. Apparently, comments in the report linking the Jupiters to the Cuban Missile Crisis negotiations touched a nerve with Dean Rusk. He instructed Assistant Secretary Tyler to inform U.S. embassies in Europe that Kissinger had no official role, that they should not help him meet high-level officials, that he did not represent the “Adm’s views,” and that “we want to discourage him,” although, as a “distinguished professor,” he should be “treated with courtesy and friendliness.” Consequently, Tyler sent that same day an “eyes only” <a href="https://www.jfklibrary.org/asset-viewer/archives/JFKNSF/321/JFKNSF-321-002">telegram</a> to U.S. ambassadors reminding them of Kissinger’s non-official status. Rusk did not explain to Tyler what Kissinger had done to irritate him. But with his interest in dispelling rumors of a secret deal, Rusk was probably irked not only by the thinking of Italian officials but also by the fact that other State Department officials, including code clerks, would see the Embassy telegram, as limited as its distribution was.<a href="#_edn17" name="_ednref17">[17]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 23</p></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29795-document-23-us-embassy-turkey-telegram-780-state-department-17-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy Turkey telegram 780 to State Department, 17 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-17T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 17, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 2047, 782.56311/1-1763</p>
</div>
          </div>eld--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 2047, 782.56311/1-1763</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In light of the Fanfani visit to the U.S., and the likelihood that the Jupiter-Polaris arrangement would be publicized, the State Department asked Hare for his estimate of Turkish reactions. Hare reported that Erkin was “perturbed” that an “impression” was being created that Turkey was “trailing behind Italians” on the Jupiter replacement issue. With a Senate debate forthcoming on the matter, Erkin did not want it to look like he was “sleeping at [the] switch” but also did not “want [to] get out in front.” Not knowing what would be said in Washington, he was not sure what “line” to take. Erkin and Hare agreed that Erkin could say in Parliament that the government was “fully informed” and would take a position that was consistent with “Turkish best interests.” Turkish officials would continue to be concerned by the appearance that they had “been <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d388">outdistanced</a> by the Italians.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 24</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29796-document-24-gti-office-near-eastern-and-south-asian-affairs-office-greek-turkish-and" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">GTI – [Office of Near Eastern and South Asian Affairs, Office of Greek, Turkish, and Iranian Affairs] John Bowling to EUR/W- E [Director of the Office of Western European Affairs Francis E.] Meloy, “Jupiter Missiles - Ankara,” 18 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-18T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 18, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>il/public/documents/rq33sq-t0790/Doc-24-1963-01-18.pdf.jpeg?itok=TqhAy7I1" alt="Doc-24-1963-01-18" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 24</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29796-document-24-gti-office-near-eastern-and-south-asian-affairs-office-greek-turkish-and" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">GTI – [Office of Near Eastern and South Asian Affairs, Office of Greek, Turkish, and Iranian Affairs] John Bowling to EUR/W- E [Director of the Office of Western European Affairs Francis E.] Meloy, “Jupiter Missiles - Ankara,” 18 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-18T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 18, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>d__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Compared to the negotiations in Italy, the talks with Turkish officials were complicated and slow moving.<a href="#_edn18" name="_ednref18">[18]</a> One problem was Foreign Minister Erkin’s “<a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d388">mistaken impression</a>” that Turkey would have a role in “manning” Polaris submarines. As desk officer John Bowling observed in this memo, Ambassador Hare saw that as a “grotesque” misunderstanding of the U.S. negotiating position, and the State Department’s <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d389">reply</a> rejected proposals for any Turkish role in commanding and staffing the submarines. All the same, Bowling saw the need for some Turkish involvement, including Turkish observers on the Polaris submarines “from time to time” and a “carefully phased” program of instruction in the U.S., beginning with “familiarization training” in the Polaris weapon system.</p>d__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Compared to the negotiations in Italy, the talks with Turkish officials were complicated and slow moving.<a href="#_edn18" name="_ednref18">[18]</a> One problem was Foreign Minister Erkin’s “<a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d388">mistaken impression</a>” that Turkey would have a role in “manning” Polaris submarines. As desk officer John Bowling observed in this memo, Ambassador Hare saw that as a “grotesque” misunderstanding of the U.S. negotiating position, and the State Department’s <a href="https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1961-63v16/d389">reply</a> rejected proposals for any Turkish role in commanding and staffing the submarines. All the same, Bowling saw the need for some Turkish involvement, including Turkish observers on the Polaris submarines “from time to time” and a “carefully phased” program of instruction in the U.S., beginning with “familiarization training” in the Polaris weapon system.</p>Turkish observers on the Polaris submarines “from time to time” and a “carefully phased” program of instruction in the U.S., beginning with “familiarization training” in the Polaris weapon system.</p>

<p>Other complications involved the provision of F-104Gs, including Turkey’s request to increase the number of fighter-bombers in the first squadron<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—</span></span></span>which Bowling wrote was “literally impossible”<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—</span></span></span>and the U.S. inability to provide a delivery date for the second squadron. He suggested that the U.S. encourage more progress with Ankara by providing information on the state of the negotiations with Italy. Optimistically, Bowling thought it “possible” to reach a “satisfactory solution in ten days or so.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
          </div>

</div>
  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="vi-backgrounders-for-the-media-and-cinceur" class="entity-list-title">VI. Backgrounders for the Media and CINCEUR </h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rq33sq-t0790/Doc-25-1963-01-17.pdf.jpeg?itok=m_fFr0Cw" alt="Doc-25-1963-01-17" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">Turkish observers on the Polaris submarines “from time to time” and a “carefully phased” program of instruction in the U.S., beginning with “familiarization training” in the Polaris weapon system.</p>

<p>Other complications involved the provision of F-104Gs, including Turkey’s request to increase the number of fighter-bombers in the first squadron<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—</span></span></span>which Bowling wrote was “literally impossible”<span style="font-size:14.0pt"><span style="line-height:107%"><span style="font-family:&quot;Times New Roman&quot;,serif">—</span></span></span>and the U.S. inability to provide a delivery date for the second squadron. He suggested that the U.S. encourage more progress with Ankara by providing information on the state of the negotiations with Italy. Optimistically, Bowling thought it “possible” to reach a “satisfactory solution in ten days or so.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
          </div>

</div>
  <div class="entity-list">

</div>
          </div>il/public/documents/rq33sq-t0790/Doc-25-1963-01-17.pdf.jpeg?itok=m_fFr0Cw" alt="Doc-25-1963-01-17" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 25</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29797-document-25-state-department-circular-telegram-1250-us-embassy-rome-et-al-18-january" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">State Department circular telegram 1250 to U.S. Embassy Rome et al., 18 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-18T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 18, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.2 Submarines Polaris etc. 1962-1964</p>
</div>
          </div>ld__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.2 Submarines Polaris etc. 1962-1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>This message, intended “only” for Ambassador Reinhardt, included information for use in backgrounders for officials and journalists, but only with the Department’s consent. It included comparisons of the Jupiter and Polaris missiles, a brief discussion of possible targeting arrangements for Polaris, and options for the possible timing of the introduction of Polaris and the phase out of Jupiter missiles. One point concerned “Equating of Italy and Turkey with Cuba.” U.S. officials were advised to make no comment on the matter, but if raised, officials should observe that when the Soviets equated missiles in Cuba with Jupiters in Italy and Turkey “we absolutely refused [to] accept any such comparison or deal.” A version of these points would soon go to Fanfani’s foreign policy adviser, Carlo Marchiori.<a href="#_edn19" name="_ednref19">[19]</a></p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-19T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 19, 1963</time>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 26</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29798-document-26-joint-chiefs-staff-message-us-cinceur-commander-chief-european-command" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Joint Chiefs of Staff message to U.S. CINCEUR [Commander in Chief European Command], “Rationale of the JUPITER Decision,” 19 January 1963, Top Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-19T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 19, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>d__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>That the Commander of U.S. European Command (CINCEUR), Gen. Lyman Lemnitzer, was a critic of the Jupiter removals may have informed a Joint Chiefs of Staff decision to send him a background paper explaining the&nbsp;“rationale of the Jupiter decision”&nbsp;that could be used for discussions within NATO.<a href="#_edn20" name="_ednref20">[20]</a> The backgrounder provided information comparing the reliability, vulnerability, and survivability, among other features, of the Jupiter and Polaris missiles. While the withdrawal of Thor and Jupiter missiles reduced Western “nuclear potential,” those reductions would be offset by an increase from 350 to about 800 U.S. strategic ballistic missiles, “some of which will be assigned to NATO targets.” Further, it “may be assumed that there will be no reduction in the present expectation of timely damage to the ACE [Allied Command Europe] targets presently covered by the Jupiters.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
          </div>

          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>That the Commander of U.S. European Command (CINCEUR), Gen. Lyman Lemnitzer, was a critic of the Jupiter removals may have informed a Joint Chiefs of Staff decision to send him a background paper explaining the&nbsp;“rationale of the Jupiter decision”&nbsp;that could be used for discussions within NATO.<a href="#_edn20" name="_ednref20">[20]</a> The backgrounder provided information comparing the reliability, vulnerability, and survivability, among other features, of the Jupiter and Polaris missiles. <div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">

<div class="row">

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 27</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29799-document-27-us-embassy-italy-telegram-1451-state-department-22-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy Italy telegram 1451 to State Department, 22 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-22T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 22, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>abel">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The State Department sent a proposed statement (not yet identified) on the Jupiters to the North Atlantic Council (NAC) for Fanfani to consider, but the Italian prime minister was holding it “exclusively” at his office, according to this U.S. Embassy telegram. Fanfani was involved in “delicate maneuverings” on the Jupiters with the Socialists and other “center-left groups,” and leaks by “indiscreet” Foreign Office officials could harm the talks.</p>

<p>Concerning President Kennedy’s proposed remarks on the Jupiters for a press conference, Fanfani understood that Kennedy had to tell them something but hoped that he would “say as little as possible.” According to Fanfani, “too much talk and too many details will give ammunition to communists and left wing” socialists. When Kennedy held his press <a href="https://www.jfklibrary.org/archives/other-resources/john-f-kennedy-press-conferences/news-conference-47">conference</a> a few days later, he said the minimum and made only a passing reference to Jupiters.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>
</div>
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

</div>

</div>

              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 84, Rome Embassy Classified General Records 1946-1964, box 125, 430.1 Italy and IRBM and NATO 1962 1963 1964</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The State Department sent a proposed statement (not yet identified) on the Jupiters to the North Atlantic Council (NAC) for Fanfani to consider, but the Italian prime minister was holding it “exclusively” at his office, according to this U.S. Embassy telegram. Fanfani was involved in “delicate maneuverings” on the Jupiters with the Socialists and other “center-left groups,” and leaks by “indiscreet” Foreign Office officials could harm the talks.</p>

<p>Concerning President Kennedy’s proposed remarks on the Jupiters for a press conference, Fanfani understood that Kennedy had to tell them something but hoped that he would “say as little as possible.” According to Fanfani, “too much talk and too many details will give ammunition to communists and left wing” socialists. When Kennedy held his press <a href="https://www.jfklibrary.org/archives/other-resources/john-f-kennedy-press-conferences/news-conference-47">conference</a> a few days later, he said the minimum and made only a passing reference to Jupiters.</p>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 28</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29800-document-28-fanfani-diary-entry-24-january-1963-excerpt" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">Fanfani Diary Entry, 24 January 1963 (excerpt)</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-24T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 24, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>Archivio Storico del Senato della repubblica Italiana, Amintore Fanfani, Diari, vol. IV, 1960-1963 (translation by Leopoldo Nuti)</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>In his diary, Fanfani recorded that the Council of Ministers had approved his report on the talks with President Kennedy and the recommendation to withdraw the Jupiters on April 1, 1963. The Ministries of Foreign Affairs and Defense would have a “mandate” to implement the withdrawal. As agreed, the Jupiters would be replaced by Polaris, and the latter would not be based in Italy. Italy would support a NATO multilateral force without the tripartite directorate that French president Charles de Gaulle had espoused.</p>
</div>

</div>il/public/documents/rq33sq-t0790/Doc-28-1963-01-24.pdf.jpeg?itok=u_sklEy6" alt="Doc-28-1963-01-24" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-24T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 24, 1963</time>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">

    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Executive Secretariat Conference Files, box 308, CF 2221</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The Embassy reported on the Council of Ministers decisions and the related communique. The latter made only general references to “modernization of arms of alliance” without mentioning Jupiters or Polaris, which was probably Fanfani’s preference. According to Reinhardt, Fanfani told him that the Council had “unanimously approved” the agreement that he and Kennedy had reached.</p>

    <h3 id="viii-loose-ends-turkey-nato-and-inconvenient-questions-from-the-us-senate" class="entity-list-title">VIII. Loose Ends: Turkey, NATO, and Inconvenient Questions from the U.S. Senate</h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rq33sq-t0790/Doc-30-1963-01-24.pdf.jpeg?itok=29wzEcUK" alt="Doc-30-1963-01-24" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 30</p>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The Embassy reported on the Council of Ministers decisions and the related communique. The latter made only general references to “modernization of arms of alliance” without mentioning Jupiters or Polaris, which was probably Fanfani’s preference. According to Reinhardt, Fanfani told him that the Council had “unanimously approved” the agreement that he and Kennedy had reached.</p>

  <div class="entity-list">
    <h3 id="viii-loose-ends-turkey-nato-and-inconvenient-questions-from-the-us-senate" class="entity-list-title">VIII. Loose Ends: Turkey, NATO, and Inconvenient Questions from the U.S. Senate</h3>

<div class="field field--name-field-documents field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__items">
              <div class="field__item"><article class="media media--type-document media--view-mode-document-list">

<div class="row">
              <div class="col-auto thumbnail-column">

<div class="field field--name-thumbnail field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/thumbnail/public/documents/rq33sq-t0790/Doc-30-1963-01-24.pdf.jpeg?itok=29wzEcUK" alt="Doc-30-1963-01-24" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-thumbnail" />

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 30</p>
</div>>

</div>

</div>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 30</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29802-document-30-state-department-telegram-834-us-embassy-turkey-24-january-1963" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">State Department telegram 834 to U.S. Embassy Turkey, 24 January 1963</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-24T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 24, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 2047, 782.5611/1-2463</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>The early delivery of F-104G fighter-bombers was a crucial element in the negotiations with Turkey over the Jupiters, and U.S. planning for their transfer was moving forward. The State Department nevertheless wanted Turkish authorities to understand that the timing of the deliveries “will depend on such progress in negotiations that it is clear [that the Government of Turkey] will agree to dismantle JUPITERS.”</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>

<div class="col fields-column">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-section field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Document 31</p>
</div>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29803-document-31-us-embassy-france-polto-879-state-department-25-january-1963-secret" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">U.S. Embassy France POLTO 879 to State Department, 25 January 1963, Secret</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-25T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 25, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 1498, 711.5611/1-2563</p>
</div>
          </div>ield--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p>RG 59, Central Decimal Files, 1960-1963, box 1498, 711.5611/1-2563</p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>Kennedy’s press statements and announcements by Italy and Turkey of agreements with the U.S. on the Jupiters put the U.S. Mission to NATO in an awkward position because, as Ambassador Finletter pointed out in this telegram, “most NAC members learned of withdrawal of Jupiters from press.” Moreover, an internal memo to NATO’s Secretary General complained about the U.S. failure to consult with the Alliance. Ambassador Finletter here asked the Department to provide a statement that he could make to the NAC at its January 30 meeting.</p>
</div>

</div>

</div>
  </article>

<div class="field field--name-field-title field--type-string field--label-hidden field__item"><a href="/document/29804-document-32-briefing-cuban-developments-us-senate-foreign-relations-committee" hreflang="en"><strong class=" wrapped-title raw-title">“Briefing on Cuban Developments,” U.S. Senate Foreign Relations Committee, Subcommittee on American Republics Affairs, 25 January 1963 (excerpt from published hearings)</strong></a></div>

<div class="field field--name-field-date field--type-datetime field--label-hidden field__item"><time datetime="1963-01-25T12:00:00Z" class="datetime">Jan 25, 1963</time>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-source field--type-text-long field--label-inline">
    <div class="field__label">Source</div>
              <div class="field__item"><p><a href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/digital-national-security-archive">Digital National Security Archive</a></p>
</div>
          </div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p>During a briefing on Cuban developments to the Senate Committee on Foreign Relations’ Subcommittee on American Republics Affairs, Secretary of State Rusk denied that there was any connection between the removal of the Jupiters and the solution of the Caribbean crisis, presenting the withdrawal instead as part of the “entire program of modernization of NATO forces,” together, in the Italian case, with the substitution of Corporal missiles with Sergeants (see PDF pages 23-26).</p>
</div>
